<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12431/2019/" target="_parent">Microphysics of summer clouds in central West Antarctica simulated by the Polar Weather Research and Forecasting  Model (WRF) and the Antarctic Mesoscale Prediction  System (AMPS)</a>
<div class="authors">Keith M. Hines, David H. Bromwich, Sheng-Hung Wang, Israel Silber, Johannes Verlinde, and Dan Lubin</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12431–12454, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12431-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12431-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_73236" data-hide=".short_summary_button_73236" data-show=".short_summary_73236">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_73236" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We explore how well clouds are represented in numerical weather prediction over Antarctica, a very difficult environment for field programs where few studies have been conducted. Fortunately, a 2015–2017 field program for West Antarctica supplied observations. We achieve promising results with newer, more advanced cloud schemes. We need to understand the role of clouds and precipitation in the maintenance of the Antarctic ice mass to understand and predict sea level change over the 21st century.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12455/2019/" target="_parent">Common volume satellite studies of polar mesospheric clouds with Odin/OSIRIS tomography and AIM/CIPS nadir imaging</a>
<div class="authors">Lina Broman, Susanne Benze, Jörg Gumbel, Ole Martin Christensen, and Cora E. Randall</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12455–12475, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12455-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12455-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_71993" data-hide=".short_summary_button_71993" data-show=".short_summary_71993">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_71993" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Combining satellite observations of polar mesospheric clouds are complicated due to satellite geometry and measurement technique. In this study, tomographic limb observations are compared to observations from a nadir-viewing satellite using a common volume approach. We present a technique that overcomes differences in scattering conditions and observation geometry. The satellites show excellent agreement, which lays the basis for future insights into horizontal and vertical cloud processes.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12495/2019/" target="_parent">Foreign influences on tropospheric ozone over East Asia through global atmospheric transport</a>
<div class="authors">Han Han, Jane Liu, Huiling Yuan, Tijian Wang, Bingliang Zhuang, and Xun Zhang</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12495–12514, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12495-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12495-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_74569" data-hide=".short_summary_button_74569" data-show=".short_summary_74569">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_74569" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                In the East Asian middle and upper troposphere, foreign ozone is 0.8–4.8 times more than its native counterpart in all the seasons. At the East Asian surface, the annual mean concentrations of foreign ozone and native ozone are comparable, being approximately 20 ppbv. The seasonal and interannual variations in foreign ozone over East Asia are closely related to the East Asian monsoon.

<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12477–12494, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12477-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12477-2019,</span> 2019</div>

<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Air masses at the Schneefernerhaus mountain site at Zugspitze Mountain, Germany, were classified with respect to the atmospheric layer from which they originated and their degree of pollution. Measurements of several gases, particulate matter, and standard meteorological quantities indicated that polluted air was lifted to the site in 31 % of cases and clean air descended to the site in approximately 14 % cases while most of the remaining cases were ambiguous.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12413/2019/" target="_parent">Estimating ground-level CO concentrations across China based on the national monitoring network and MOPITT: potentially overlooked CO hotspots in the Tibetan Plateau</a>
<div class="authors">Dongren Liu, Baofeng Di, Yuzhou Luo, Xunfei Deng, Hanyue Zhang, Fumo Yang, Michael L. Grieneisen, and Yu Zhan</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12413–12430, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12413-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12413-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75200" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75200" data-show=".short_summary_75200">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75200" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The spatiotemporal distributions of daily ground-level CO concentrations across China during 2013–2016 are derived by fusing the data from remote sensing and ground monitoring. The population–weighted CO was predicted to be 0.99 ± 0.30 mg m<sup>−3</sup> and showed a decreasing trend of −0.021 ± 0.004 mg m<sup>−3</sup> per year. The CO pollution was the most severe in the North China Plain. The hotspots in the Tibetan Plateau overlooked by the remote sensing were depicted by the data-fusion approach.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12397/2019/" target="_parent">Photomineralization mechanism changes the ability of dissolved organic matter to activate cloud droplets and to nucleate ice crystals</a>
<div class="authors">Nadine Borduas-Dedekind, Rachele Ossola, Robert O. David, Lin S. Boynton, Vera Weichlinger, Zamin A. Kanji, and Kristopher McNeill</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12397–12412, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12397-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12397-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_76135" data-hide=".short_summary_button_76135" data-show=".short_summary_76135">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_76135" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                During atmospheric transport, dissolved organic matter (DOM) within aqueous aerosols undergoes photochemistry. We find that photochemical processing of DOM increases its ability to form cloud droplets but decreases its ability to form ice crystals over a simulated 4.6 days in the atmosphere. A photomineralization mechanism involving the loss of organic carbon and the production of organic acids, CO and CO<sub>2</sub> explains the observed changes and affects the liquid-water-to-ice ratio in clouds.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-821/" target="_parent">Evaluation of the CAMS global atmospheric trace gas reanalysis 2003–2016 using aircraft campaign observations</a>
<div class="authors">Yuting Wang, Yong-Feng Ma, Henk Eskes, Antje Inness, Johannes Flemming, and Guy P. Brasseur</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-821,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-821,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80029" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80029" data-show=".short_summary_80029">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80029" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The paper presents an evaluation of the CAMS global reanalysis of reactive gases performed for the period 2003–2016. The evaluation is performed by comparing  concentrations of chemical species gathered during airborne field campaigns with calculated values. The reanalysis reproduces successfully the observed concentrations of ozone and carbon monoxide but generally underestimates the abundance of hydrocarbons. Large discrepancies exist for fast-reacting radicals such as OH and HO<sub>2</sub>.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-770/" target="_parent">Diurnal cycle of clouds extending above the tropical tropopause observed by spaceborne lidar</a>
<div class="authors">Thibaut Dauhut, Vincent Noël, and Iris-Amata Dion</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-770,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-770,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79763" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79763" data-show=".short_summary_79763">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79763" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We document for the first time the diurnal cycle of the clouds in the tropical stratosphere, using the measurements from the lidar onboard the international space station. The stratospheric clouds are concentrated over the convective centers. Their cloud fraction is minimal and limited to the vicinity of the tropopause during daytime. It presents two maxima: one in the early night and one shortly after midnight, when clouds also extend deeper in stratosphere.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12325/2019/" target="_parent">Growth in mid-monsoon dry phases over the Indian region: prevailing influence of anthropogenic aerosols</a>
<div class="authors">Rohit Chakraborty, Bijay Kumar Guha, Shamitaksha Talukdar, Madineni Venkat Ratnam, and Animesh Maitra</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12325–12341, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12325-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12325-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_73799" data-hide=".short_summary_button_73799" data-show=".short_summary_73799">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_73799" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The present study investigates the plausible aspects which influence the probability of drought occurrences over three Indian regions during the southwest Asian mid-monsoon period. The investigation reveals that an increasing tendency of dry day frequency (DDF) over urbanized regions in the last few decades has significant association with the abundance of anthropogenic aerosols. Additionally, future projections of DDF indicate a five-fold rise which can be a crucial concern for policy makers.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12343/2019/" target="_parent">Relative impact of aerosol, soil moisture, and orography perturbations on deep convection</a>
<div class="authors">Linda Schneider, Christian Barthlott, Corinna Hoose, and Andrew I. Barrett</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12343–12359, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12343-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12343-2019,</span> 2019</div>

<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75117" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                This study addresses the relative impact of orography, soil moisture, and aerosols on precipitation over Germany in different weather regimes. We find that the impact of these perturbations is higher for weak than for strong large-scale forcing. Furthermore, aerosols and soil moisture are both of similar importance for precipitation forecasting, which indicates that their inclusion in operational ensemble forecasting should be assessed in the future.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12361/2019/" target="_parent">Contrasting effects of CO<sub>2</sub> fertilization, land-use change and warming on seasonal amplitude of Northern Hemisphere CO<sub>2</sub> exchange</a>
<div class="authors">Ana Bastos, Philippe Ciais, Frédéric Chevallier, Christian Rödenbeck, Ashley P. Ballantyne, Fabienne Maignan, Yi Yin, Marcos Fernández-Martínez, Pierre Friedlingstein, Josep Peñuelas, Shilong L. Piao, Stephen Sitch, William K. Smith, Xuhui Wang, Zaichun Zhu, Vanessa Haverd, Etsushi Kato, Atul K. Jain, Sebastian Lienert, Danica Lombardozzi, Julia E. M. S. Nabel, Philippe Peylin, Benjamin Poulter, and Dan Zhu</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12361–12375, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12361-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12361-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75241" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75241" data-show=".short_summary_75241">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75241" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Here we show that land-surface models improved their ability to simulate the increase in the amplitude of seasonal CO<sub>2</sub>-cycle exchange (SCA<sub>NBP</sub>) by ecosystems compared to estimates by two atmospheric inversions. We find a dominant role of vegetation growth over boreal Eurasia to the observed increase in SCA<sub>NBP</sub>, strongly driven by CO<sub>2</sub> fertilization, and an overall negative effect of temperature on SCA<sub>NBP</sub>. Biases can be explained by the sensitivity of simulated microbial respiration to temperature.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12377/2019/" target="_parent">Wintertime aerosol measurements during the Chilean Coastal Orographic Precipitation Experiment</a>
<div class="authors">Sara Lynn Fults, Adam K. Massmann, Aldo Montecinos, Elisabeth Andrews, David E. Kingsmill, Justin R. Minder, René D. Garreaud, and Jefferson R. Snider</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12377–12396, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12377-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12377-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_74854" data-hide=".short_summary_button_74854" data-show=".short_summary_74854">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_74854" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We analyze wintertime aerosol measurements from the central Chilean Pacific coast. The averaged aerosol particle concentration at our site (D &gt; 0.01 μm) is larger than at a site on the Californian Pacific coast. Additionally, size distributions sampled during intervals of onshore flow are used to parameterize aerosol properties relevant to cloud and precipitation processes. We anticipate that modeling of wintertime Chilean coastal rain events will benefit from the parameterizations we present.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-861/" target="_parent">Protein aggregates nucleate ice: the example of apoferritin</a>
<div class="authors">María Cascajo-Castresana, Robert O. David, Maiara A. Iriarte-Alonso, Alexander M. Bittner, and Claudia Marcolli</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-861,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-861,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80369" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80369" data-show=".short_summary_80369">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80369" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Atmospheric ice-nucleating particles are rare but relevant for cloud glaciation. A source of particles that nucleate ice above −15 °C is biological material including some proteins. Here we show that proteins of very diverse functions and structures can nucleate ice. Among these, the iron storage protein apoferritin stands out, with activity up to −4 °C. We show that its activity does not stem from correctly assembled proteins but from misfolded protein monomers or oligomers and aggregates.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-807/" target="_parent">Formation of highly oxygenated organic molecules from chlorine atom 
initiated oxidation of alpha-pinene</a>
<div class="authors">Yonghong Wang, Matthieu Riva, Hongbin Xie, Liine Heikkinen, Simon Schallhart, Qiaozhi Zha, Chao Yan, Xucheng He, Otso Peräkylä, and Mikael Ehn</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-807,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-807,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79961" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79961" data-show=".short_summary_79961">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79961" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Chamber experiments were conducted with alpha-pinene and chlorine under low and high nitrogen oxide (NO<sub>x</sub>) conditions.We estimated the HOM yields from chlorine-initiated oxidation of alpha-pinene under low-NOX conditions to be around 1.8 %, though with a  uncertainty range (0.8–4 %) due to lack of suitable calibration methods. Our study clearly demonstrates that the chlorine atom initiated oxidation of alpha-pinene can produce low volatility organic compounds.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-854/" target="_parent">Fine particle characterization in a coastal city in China: composition, sources, and impacts of industrial emissions</a>
<div class="authors">Lu Lei, Conghui Xie, Dawei Wang, Yao He, Qingqing Wang, Wei Zhou, Wei Hu, Pingqing Fu, Yong Chen, Xiaole Pan, Zifa Wang, Douglas R. Worsnop, and Yele Sun</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-854,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-854,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 1 comment)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80290" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80290" data-show=".short_summary_80290">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80290" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We characterized aerosol composition and sources near two steel plants in a coastal region in fall and spring seasons. Our results showed substantially different aerosol composition and sources between the two seasons. We observed significant impacts of steel plants emissions on aerosol chemistry nearby, and found that aerosol particles emitted from the steel plants were dominated by ammonium sulfate/bisulfate, and NO<sub>x</sub>/CO and NO<sub>x</sub>/SO<sub>2</sub> were distinct from those in the absence of industrial plumes.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-717/" target="_parent">Supercooled Drizzle Development in Response to Semi-Coherent
Vertical Velocity Fluctuations Within an Orographic Layer Cloud</a>
<div class="authors">Adam Majewski and Jeffrey R. French</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-717,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-717,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 2 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79190" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79190" data-show=".short_summary_79190">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79190" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The study reports formation of super-cooled drizzle drops in response to repeating kilometer-wide up- and down- drafts within a mixed-phase, mountain layer cloud containing very little ice despite cold cloud top temperatures (T ~ −30 °C). The discrete, embedded hydrometeor growth layers and downwind transition to drizzle production at cloud top indicates the relative importance of kinematic mechanisms in determining the location of precipitation development in cloud.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-864/" target="_parent">Effects of Black Carbon Mitigation on Arctic Climate</a>
<div class="authors">Thomas Kühn, Kaarle Kupiainen, Tuuli Miinalainen, Harri Kokkola, Ville-Veikko Paunu, Anton Laakso, Juha Tonttila, Rita Van Dingenen, Kati Kulovesi, Niko Karvosenoja, and Kari E. J. Lehtinen</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-864,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-864,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80378" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80378" data-show=".short_summary_80378">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80378" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We investigate the effects of black carbon mitigation on Arctic climate and on human health, accounting for the concurrent reduction of other aerosol species. While black carbon is attributed a net warming effect on climate, most other aerosol species cool the planet. We find that the direct radiative effect induces cooling, while aerosol-cloud effects offset this cooling and introduce large uncertainties. Furthermore, the reduced aerosol emissions reduce human mortality considerably.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-735/" target="_parent">Ice-supersaturated  air  masses  in  the  northern mid-latitudes from regular in-situ   observations   by   passenger   aircraft: vertical distribution, seasonality and tropospheric fingerprint</a>
<div class="authors">Andreas Petzold, Patrick Neis, Mihal Rütimann, Susanne Rohs, Florian Berkes, Herman G. J. Smit, Martina Krämer, Nicole Spelten, Peter Spichtinger, Philippe Nedelec, and Andreas Wahner</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-735,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-735,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 1 comment)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79264" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79264" data-show=".short_summary_79264">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79264" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The first analysis of 15 years of global-scale water vapour and relative humidity observations by passenger aircraft in the MOZAIC programme resolves detailed features of water vapour and ice-supersaturated air in the mid-latitude tropopause. Key results provide in-depth insight into seasonal and regional variability and chemical signatures of ice-supersaturated air masses, including trend analyses, and a close link to cirrus clouds and their highly important effects on climate.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-769/" target="_parent">Measurements to determine mixing state of black carbon emitted
from the 2017/2018 California wildfires and urban Los Angeles</a>
<div class="authors">Joseph Ko, Trevor Krasowsky, and George Ban-Weiss</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-769,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-769,</span> 2019</div>

<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79757" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79757" data-show=".short_summary_79757">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79757" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Black carbon (BC) is often cited as the second strongest climate forcing pollutant in the atmosphere, after carbon dioxide. Here, we aim to observe how BC physical properties, particularly coating thickness, evolve in the atmosphere. By comparing BC-containing particles from different sources and with different aging timescales, we find that the physical properties of BC are highly dependent on atmospheric context. Of particular interest, we observe that an aging timescale of less than one day.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-790/" target="_parent">Ceilometers as planetary boundary layer detectors and a
corrective tool for ECMWF and COSMO NWP models</a>
<div class="authors">Leenes Uzan, Smadar Egert, Pavel Khain, Yoav Levi, Elyakom Vladislavsky, and Pinhas Alpert</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-790,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-790,</span> 2019</div>

<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79877" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79877" data-show=".short_summary_79877">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79877" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Detection of the planetary boundary layer (PBL) height is crucial to various fields, from air pollution assessment to weather prediction. We examined the diurnal summer PBL height by eight ceilometers in Israel, radiosonde profiles, the global IFS, and regional COSMO models. Our analysis utilized the bulk Richardson number method, the parcel method, and the wavelet covariance transform method. A novel correction tool to improve model results against in-situ ceilometer measurements is introduced.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12209/2019/" target="_parent">A large contribution of anthropogenic organo-nitrates to secondary organic aerosol in the Alberta oil sands</a>
<div class="authors">Alex K. Y. Lee, Max G. Adam, John Liggio, Shao-Meng Li, Kun Li, Megan D. Willis, Jonathan P. D. Abbatt, Travis W. Tokarek, Charles A. Odame-Ankrah, Hans D. Osthoff, Kevin Strawbridge, and Jeffery R. Brook</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12209–12219, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12209-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12209-2019,</span> 2019</div>

<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                This work provides the first direct field evidence that anthropogenic organo-nitrate contributed up to half of secondary organic aerosol (SOA) mass that was freshly produced within the emission plumes of oil sands facilities in Alberta, Canada. The findings illustrate the central role of organo-nitrate in SOA production from the oil and gas industry, with relevance for other urban and industrial regions with significant intermediate-volatility organic compounds (IVOCs) and NO<sub>x</sub> emissions.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12309/2019/" target="_parent">Improved FTIR retrieval strategy for HCFC-22 (CHClF<sub>2</sub>), comparisons with in situ and satellite datasets with the support of models, and determination of its long-term  trend above Jungfraujoch</a>
<div class="authors">Maxime Prignon, Simon Chabrillat, Daniele Minganti, Simon O'Doherty, Christian Servais, Gabriele Stiller, Geoffrey C. Toon, Martin K. Vollmer, and Emmanuel Mahieu</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12309–12324, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12309-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12309-2019,</span> 2019</div>

<div class="widget dark-border">

<div class="content">
                                Hydrochlorofluorocarbons (HCFCs) are the first, but temporary, substitution products for the strong ozone-depleting chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs). In this work, we present and validate an improved method to retrieve the most abundant HCFC in the atmosphere, allowing its evolution to be monitored independently in the troposphere and stratosphere. <a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12235/2019/" target="_parent">Controls on the water vapor isotopic composition near the surface of tropical oceans and role of boundary layer mixing processes</a>
<div class="authors">Camille Risi, Joseph Galewsky, Gilles Reverdin, and Florent Brient</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12235–12260, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12235-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12235-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75252" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75252" data-show=".short_summary_75252">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75252" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Water molecules can be light (one oxygen atom and two hydrogen atoms) or heavy (one hydrogen atom is replaced by a deuterium atom). These different molecules are called water isotopes. The isotopic composition of water vapor can potentially provide information about physical processes along the water cycle, but the factors controlling it are complex. As a first step, we propose an equation  to predict the water vapor isotopic composition near the surface of tropical oceans.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12295/2019/" target="_parent">Relative humidity and O<sub>3</sub> concentration as two prerequisites  for sulfate formation</a>
<div class="authors">Yanhua Fang, Chunxiang Ye, Junxia Wang, Yusheng Wu, Min Hu, Weili Lin, Fanfan Xu, and Tong Zhu</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12295–12307, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12295-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12295-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75402" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75402" data-show=".short_summary_75402">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75402" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Year-long observations of PM<sub>2.5</sub>, gaseous pollutants, and meteorological parameters in Beijing were analysed to investigate sulfate formation. RH and O<sub>3</sub> concentrations above thresholds of 45 % and 35 ppb, respectively, greatly accelerated sulfate formation. Ambient changes in RH and O<sub>3</sub> contributed to variations in sulfate formation among different seasons and pollution levels. A shift from gas-phase to multiphase SO<sub>2</sub> oxidation contributed to fast sulfate formation under polluted conditions.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12195/2019/" target="_parent">Substantial ozone enhancement over the North China Plain from increased biogenic emissions due to heat waves and land cover in summer 2017</a>
<div class="authors">Mingchen Ma, Yang Gao, Yuhang Wang, Shaoqing Zhang, L. Ruby Leung, Cheng Liu, Shuxiao Wang, Bin Zhao, Xing Chang, Hang Su, Tianqi Zhang, Lifang Sheng, Xiaohong Yao, and Huiwang Gao</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12195–12207, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12195-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12195-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75786" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75786" data-show=".short_summary_75786">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75786" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Ozone pollution has become severe in China, and extremely high ozone episodes occurred in summer 2017 over the North China Plain. While meteorology impacts are clear, we find that enhanced biogenic emissions, previously ignored by the community, driven by high vapor pressure deficit, land cover change and urban landscape contribute substantially to ozone formation. This study has significant implications for ozone pollution control with more frequent heat waves and urbanization growth in future.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12261/2019/" target="_parent">NH<sub>3</sub> emissions from large point sources derived from CrIS and IASI satellite observations</a>
<div class="authors">Enrico Dammers, Chris A. McLinden, Debora Griffin, Mark W. Shephard, Shelley Van Der Graaf, Erik Lutsch, Martijn Schaap, Yonatan Gainairu-Matz, Vitali Fioletov, Martin Van Damme, Simon Whitburn, Lieven Clarisse, Karen Cady-Pereira, Cathy Clerbaux, Pierre Francois Coheur, and Jan Willem Erisman</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12261–12293, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12261-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12261-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_76509" data-hide=".short_summary_button_76509" data-show=".short_summary_76509">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_76509" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Ammonia is an essential molecule in the environment, but at its current levels it is unsustainable. However, the emissions are highly uncertain. We explore the use of satellites to estimate the ammonia lifetime and emissions around point sources to help improve the budget. The same method applied to different satellite instruments shows consistent results. Comparison to the emission inventories shows that those are underestimating emissions of point sources by on average a factor of 2.5.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-753/" target="_parent">Improving the prediction of an atmospheric chemistry transport model using gradient boosted regression trees</a>
<div class="authors">Peter D. Ivatt and Mathew J. Evans</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-753,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-753,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79500" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79500" data-show=".short_summary_79500">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79500" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We investigate the potential of using a decision tree algorithm to identify and correct the tropospheric ozone bias in a chemical transport model. We train the algorithm on 2010–2015 ground and column observation data, and test the algorithm on the 2016–2017 data using the ground data as well as independent flight data. We find the algorithm is successfully able to identify and correct the bias, improving the model performance which could improve the performance of air quality forecasts.

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-747/" target="_parent">Modelling the potential impacts of the recent, unexpected increase in CFC-11 emissions on total column ozone recovery</a>
<div class="authors">James Keeble, N. Luke Abraham, Alexander T. Archibald, Martyn P. Chipperfield, Sandip Dhomse, Paul T. Griffiths, and John A. Pyle</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-747,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-747,</span> 2019</div>

<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_79374" data-hide=".short_summary_button_79374" data-show=".short_summary_79374">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_79374" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                The Montreal Protocol was agreed in 1987 to limit and then stop the production of man-made CFCs, which destroy stratospheric ozone. As a result, the atmospheric abundances of CFCs are now declining in the atmosphere. However, the atmospheric abundance of CFC-11 is not declining as expected under complete compliance with the Montreal Protocol. Using the UM-UKCA chemistry climate model, we explore the impact of future unregulated production of CFC-11 on the timing of ozone recovery.

<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-844,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-844,</span> 2019</div>

<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80234" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We address, on the molecular level, the potential, and consequences of, surface properties variations of aerosol particles in the atmosphere. Aerosol surface aging changes the interfacial chemistry, giving rise to marked variations the ice nucleation ability of the surface. <a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12175/2019/" target="_parent">Laboratory study of the heterogeneous ice nucleation  on black-carbon-containing aerosol</a>
<div class="authors">Leonid Nichman, Martin Wolf, Paul Davidovits, Timothy B. Onasch, Yue Zhang, Doug R. Worsnop, Janarjan Bhandari, Claudio Mazzoleni, and Daniel J. Cziczo</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12175–12194, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12175-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12175-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_71214" data-hide=".short_summary_button_71214" data-show=".short_summary_71214">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_71214" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Previous studies showed widespread ice nucleation activity of soot. In this systematic study we investigated the factors that affect the heterogeneous ice nucleation activity of soot surrogates in the cirrus cloud regime. Our observations are consistent with an ice nucleation mechanism of pore condensation followed by freezing. The results show significant variations in ice nucleation activity as a function of size, morphology, and surface chemistry of the black-carbon-containing particles.
                            </div>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12163/2019/" target="_parent">Interactions between aerosol organic components and liquid water content during haze episodes in Beijing</a>
<div class="authors">Xiaoxiao Li, Shaojie Song, Wei Zhou, Jiming Hao, Douglas R. Worsnop, and Jingkun Jiang</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12163–12174, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12163-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12163-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_75533" data-hide=".short_summary_button_75533" data-show=".short_summary_75533">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_75533" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Aerosol liquid water is ubiquitous in ambient aerosol. Using long-term aerosol chemical composition to model the aerosol water in Beijing, we found that water absorbed by organics contributes a significant fraction to the total aerosol water. We emphasize the hygroscopicity of organics is highly variable and should be taken into consideration in modelling. A positive feedback loop between organic hygroscopicity and aerosol water was found as one of the driving factors of severe haze in Beijing.
                            </div>
<div><a class="show-hide triangle" data-hide=".short_summary_75533" data-show=".short_summary_button_75533" href="#">Hide</a></div>
</div>
</div> </div>

<a class="paperlist-avatar" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12163/2019/acp-19-12163-2019-avatar-web.png" target="_blank">
<img class="img-responsive" data-caption="© Authors. Distributed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 License." data-height="346" data-web="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12163/2019/acp-19-12163-2019-avatar-web.png" data-width="600" src="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12163/2019/acp-19-12163-2019-avatar-thumb80.png"/>

<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-828/" target="_parent">Cloud Condensation Nuclei properties of South Asian outflow over the northern Indian Ocean during winter</a>
<div class="authors">Vijayakumar S. Nair, Jayachandran Venugopalan Nair, Sobhan Kumar Kompalli, Mukunda M. Gogoi, and S. Suresh Babu</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-828,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-828,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80148" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80148" data-show=".short_summary_80148">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80148" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                Extensive measurements of the aerosol and cloud condensation nuclei (CCN) properties in South Asian outflow to the northern Indian Ocean were carried out as a part of ICARB-2018 experiment during winter. At high supersaturations, most of the aerosols in the south Asian outflow get activated as CCN, whereas aerosol system over the equatorial Indian Ocean is less CCN efficient even at higher supersaturations.
                            </div>
<div><a class="show-hide triangle" data-hide=".short_summary_80148" data-show=".short_summary_button_80148" href="#">Hide</a></div>

<div class="published-date"> 01 Oct 2019</div>
<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-829/" target="_parent">The isotopic composition of atmospheric nitrous oxide
observed at the high-altitude research station Jungfraujoch,
Switzerland</a>
<div class="authors">Longfei Yu, Eliza Harris, Stephan Henne, Sarah Eggleston, Martin Steinbacher, Lukas Emmenegger, Christoph Zellweger, and Joachim Mohn</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-829,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-829,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80150" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80150" data-show=".short_summary_80150">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80150" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                We observed the isotopic composition of nitrous oxide in the unpolluted air at Jungfraujoch for five years. Our results indicate a clear season pattern in the isotopic composition, corresponding with that in atmospheric nitrous oxide levels. This is most likely due to temporal variations in both emission processes and air mass sources for Jungfraujoch. Our findings are of importance to global nitrous oxide modelling and to better understanding of long-term trends in atmospheric nitrous oxide.
                            </div>
<div><a class="show-hide triangle" data-hide=".short_summary_80150" data-show=".short_summary_button_80150" href="#">Hide</a></div>

<div class="published-date"> 01 Oct 2019</div>
<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys-discuss.net/acp-2019-859/" target="_parent">Influence of vessel characteristics and atmospheric processes on the gas and  particle phase of ship emission plumes: In-situ measurements in the Mediterranean Sea and around the Arabian Peninsula</a>
<div class="authors">Siddika Celik, Frank Drewnick, Friederike Fachinger, James Brooks, Eoghan Darbyshire, Hugh Coe, Jean-Daniel Paris, Philipp G. Eger, Jan Schuladen, Ivan Tadic, Nils Friedrich, Dirk Dienhart, Bettina Hottmann, Horst Fischer, John N. Crowley, Hartwig Harder, and Stephan Borrmann</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys. Discuss., <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-859,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-2019-859,</span> 2019</div>
<div class="statusMessage"><span>Manuscript under review for ACP</span> <nobr>(discussion: open, 0 comments)</nobr></div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_80353" data-hide=".short_summary_button_80353" data-show=".short_summary_80353">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_80353" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                252 emission plumes of ships operating in the Mediterranean Sea and around the Arabian Peninsula were investigated using a comprehensive dataset of gas and submicron particle phase properties measured during the two-month shipborne AQABA field campaign in summer 2017. 
Post-measurement identification of the source vessels of the emission events and determination of the plume ages enabled the investigation of the influences of vessel characteristics and atmospheric processes on plume properties.
                            </div>
<div><a class="show-hide triangle" data-hide=".short_summary_80353" data-show=".short_summary_button_80353" href="#">Hide</a></div>
</div>

<div class="published-date"> 30 Sep 2019</div>
<a class="article-title" href="https://www.atmos-chem-phys.net/19/12141/2019/" target="_parent">Assessment of the theoretical limit in instrumental detectability of northern high-latitude methane sources using <i>δ</i><sup>13</sup>C<sub>CH4</sub> atmospheric signals</a>
<div class="authors">Thibaud Thonat, Marielle Saunois, Isabelle Pison, Antoine Berchet, Thomas Hocking, Brett F. Thornton, Patrick M. Crill, and Philippe Bousquet</div>
<div class="citation">Atmos. Chem. Phys., 19, 12141–12161, <nobr class="hide-on-mobile hide-on-tablet">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12141-2019,</nobr><span class="hide-on-desktop">https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-19-12141-2019,</span> 2019</div>
<span class="show-hide journal-contentLinkColor triangle short_summary_button_73058" data-hide=".short_summary_button_73058" data-show=".short_summary_73058">Short summary</span>
<div class="j-widget__max short_summary short_summary_73058" style="display: none">
<div class="widget dark-border">
<div class="legend journal-contentLinkColor">Short summary</div>
<div class="content">
                                This paper discusses the methane isotopic signals that could be detected at instrumental surface sites in the northern high latitudes using a 3–D chemistry transport model. Isotopic signals may be used in atmospheric inverse systems to better characterize methane emissions and changes. We show that depending on the source magnitude and the location of the site, detecting isotopic signals of specific individual sources may be challenging for the new generation of methane isotope instruments.
                            </div>
<div><a class="show-hide triangle" data-hide=".short_summary_73058" data-show=".short_summary_button_73058" href="#">Hide</a></div>
</div>

        $('#start-search').unbind('click').bind('click', function(){
            var type  = $('#search-type').val();
            var query = $('#search-query').val();
            if(type.indexOf('google') !== -1){
                window.open(type + encodeURI(query), '_blank');

                document.location.href = type + encodeURI(query);
            }
        });

                var query = $('#search-query').val();

                } else {
                    document.location.href = type + encodeURI(query);
